3. **Vertical descent**: Descend straight down to the grasp height.

To grasp firmly, close the gripper across the smaller dimension. The closing direction should align with the axis being clamped. I'll grasp across the width (0.063) — closing direction along orientation -0.042.

```python
import math

# ---- Key poses / dimensions ----
start_pose = [0.08616413764032595, 0.2586898534132921, 0.7637841868693245, 0.0]

cyl_x, cyl_y = 0.034, 0.121
grasp_z = 0.893          # cylinder center height
safe_z  = 1.35           # above cylinder top (1.142) with margin
grip_theta = -0.042      # close across the 0.063 width

place_dx = 0.30          # move 30 cm in +x, clear of door
place_x = cyl_x + place_dx
place_y = cyl_y

# ---- Phase 1: vertical lift from start to safe height (same XY) ----
p_start_lift = [start_pose[0], start_pose[1], safe_z, 0.0]
T1 = generate_linear_trajectory(
    "Vertical lift from start to safe height",
    start_pose, p_start_lift, num_points=8)
execute_trajectory(T1)
logger.info(PROGRESS + "T1 done: lifted to safe height" + ENDC)

# ---- Phase 2: lateral travel to above cylinder + rotate to grip angle ----
p_above_cyl = [cyl_x, cyl_y, safe_z, grip_theta]
T2 = generate_linear_trajectory(
    "Lateral travel to above cylinder center, aligning grip",
    p_start_lift, p_above_cyl, num_points=12)
execute_trajectory(T2)
logger.info(PROGRESS + "T2 done: above cylinder center" + ENDC)

# ---- Phase 3: vertical descent to grasp height ----
p_grasp = [cyl_x, cyl_y, grasp_z, grip_theta]
T3 = generate_linear_trajectory(
    "Vertical descent to grasp height",
    p_above_cyl, p_grasp, num_points=8)
execute_trajectory(T3)
logger.info(PROGRESS + "T3 done: at grasp height" + ENDC)

# ---- Grasp ----
close_gripper()
logger.info(PROGRESS + "Gripper closed on cylinder" + ENDC)

# ---- Phase 4: vertical lift with the cylinder ----
p_lift = [cyl_x, cyl_y, safe_z, grip_theta]
T4 = generate_linear_trajectory(
    "Vertical lift of cylinder",
    p_grasp, p_lift, num_points=8)
execute_trajectory(T4)
logger.info(PROGRESS + "T4 done: cylinder lifted" + ENDC)

# ---- Phase 5: lateral travel +0.30 x, clear of door ----
p_over_place = [place_x, place_y, safe_z, grip_theta]
T5 = generate_linear_trajectory(
    "Lateral travel to place location clear of door",
    p_lift, p_over_place, num_points=12)
execute_trajectory(T5)
logger.info(PROGRESS + "T5 done: over place location" + ENDC)

# ---- Phase 6: vertical descent to place on floor ----
p_place = [place_x, place_y, grasp_z, grip_theta]
T6 = generate_linear_trajectory(
    "Vertical descent to place cylinder",
    p_over_place, p_place, num_points=8)
execute_trajectory(T6)
logger.info(PROGRESS + "T6 done: cylinder placed" + ENDC)

# ---- Release ----
open_gripper()
logger.info(PROGRESS + "Gripper opened, cylinder released" + ENDC)

# ---- Phase 7: vertical retreat ----
p_retreat = [place_x, place_y, safe_z, grip_theta]
T7 = generate_linear_trajectory(
    "Vertical retreat after release",
    p_place, p_retreat, num_points=8)
execute_trajectory(T7)
logger.info(PROGRESS + "T7 done: retreated" + ENDC)

task_completed()
```

**Corrected cylinder position: x ≈ -0.20, y ≈ 0.40, center z ≈ 0.893.**

```python
import math

# ---- Object / pose parameters ----
start_pose = [0.09042489284866251, 0.26705833051924677, 0.7650738560278775, 0.0]

# Corrected cylinder position (directly in front of the door, toward camera)

cyl_center_z = 0.893          # grasp at center height
cyl_top_z    = 1.142          # top surface
grasp_theta  = 1.529          # align with cylinder's shorter side (length=0.032)

SAFE_Z = 1.35                 # above cylinder top (1.142) with margin, >50cm lift clearance
GRASP_Z = cyl_center_z        # descend to center for a stable grasp

# Place location: +0.30 m in +y (toward camera), away from door & its swing path
place_x, place_y = cyl_x, cyl_y + 0.30

logger.info(PROGRESS + "Attempt 3: corrected cylinder XY to (-0.20, 0.40), grasp theta=1.529" + ENDC)

# ---- Helper for a pose ----
def pose(x, y, z, theta):
    return [x, y, z, theta]

# ================= PHASE 1: APPROACH & GRASP =================

    "T1: vertical lift to safe height",
    start_pose,
    pose(start_pose[0], start_pose[1], SAFE_Z, 0.0),
    num_points=10)

# T2: Lateral travel at safe height to above cylinder, rotate to grasp orientation

    "T2: lateral travel above cylinder, rotate to grasp",
    pose(start_pose[0], start_pose[1], SAFE_Z, 0.0),
    pose(cyl_x, cyl_y, SAFE_Z, grasp_theta),
    num_points=15)

    pose(cyl_x, cyl_y, SAFE_Z, grasp_theta),
    pose(cyl_x, cyl_y, GRASP_Z, grasp_theta),
    num_points=10)

# Grasp

logger.info(PROGRESS + "Gripper closed on cylinder" + ENDC)
```

```python
# ================= PHASE 2: LIFT, MOVE, PLACE =================

# T4: Vertical lift cylinder to safe height (>50cm above top surface)

    "T4: lift cylinder to safe height",
    pose(cyl_x, cyl_y, GRASP_Z, grasp_theta),
    pose(cyl_x, cyl_y, SAFE_Z, grasp_theta),
    num_points=12)

    "T6: vertical descent to place",
```

  topple: approach the object from the side near its max height to have it fall

Negative rotation values represent clockwise rotation, and positive rotation values represent anticlockwise rotation. The rotation values should be in radians.

GRIPPER ORIENTATION DEFINITION:
- The rotation value specifies the direction of the gripper's CLOSING MOTION (i.e., the direction along which the fingers move when closing).
- The gripper fingers themselves are perpendicular to this direction.
- Therefore, to grasp an object along a given side, the rotation must be aligned with that side.

Example:
- To grasp the shorter side of an object, set rotation = angle_short.
- To grasp the longer side, set rotation = angle_long.

1. The default speed of the robot arm end-effector is 20 points per trajectory. If the total distance covered is small, keep the number of points low, as the task in sim-env has max-number-of-steps (~500-700) and each point is translated to several steps.
2. If you need to make the end-effector follow a particular trajectory more quickly, then generate fewer points for the trajectory, and vice versa.

CODE GENERATION:
When generating the code for the trajectory, do the following:
1. Describe briefly the shape of the motion trajectory required to complete the task.
2. The trajectory could be broken down into multiple steps. In that case, each trajectory step (at default speed) should contain at least 5 points. Define general functions which can be reused for the different trajectory steps whenever possible, but make sure to define new functions whenever a new motion is required. Output a step-by-step reasoning before generating the code.
3. If the trajectory is broken down into multiple steps, make sure to chain them such that the start point of trajectory_2 is the same as the end point of trajectory_1 and so on, to ensure a smooth overall trajectory. Call the execute_trajectory function after each trajectory step.
3b. For contact-rich manipulation tasks (for example handles, drawers, doors, switches, or articulated objects), prefer decomposing the motion into separate phases: high hover approach, lateral alignment at safe height, vertical descent, contact/grasp, articulation motion, and retreat. Avoid combining lateral insertion and vertical contact motions into a single diagonal approach whenever possible.
4. When defining the functions, specify the required parameters, and document them clearly in the code. Make sure to include the orientation parameter in both definition and calls (use). make sure all dimensions of caller arguments match the function definition and body
5. Do not print any of the trajectory variables, since the output will be too long.
6. Mark any code clearly with the ```python and ``` tags. No need to import any of the AVAILABLE FUNCTIONS listed above - they (and `logger`, PROGRESS, ENDC) are already injected into the Python interpreter.
If you want to print a value to reuse later, use print(...) (floats to three decimal places) rather than writing the variable name alone.
Use logger.info(PROGRESS + f"..." + ENDC) for concise status logs instead of print for routine status. NOTE: `logger` is an already-injected function/object (loguru), NOT a module - do NOT run `import logger` (it will raise ModuleNotFoundError) and do not re-initialize it.
7. Make sure all used variables in a python block are defined in this block. you can merge several blocks if appropriate. Use the provided generate_linear_trajectory helper; do not redefine it.

INITIAL PLANNING 1:
If the task requires interaction with an object part (as opposed to the object as a whole), describe which part of the object would be most suitable for the gripper to interact with.
Then, detect the necessary objects in the environment. Stop generation after this step to wait until you obtain the printed outputs from the detect_object function calls.
INITIAL PLANNING 2:
Then, output Python code to decide which object to interact with, if there are multiple instances of the same object.
Then, describe how best to approach the object (for example, approaching the midpoint of the object, or one of its edges, etc.), depending on the nature of the task, or the object dimensions, etc.

For thin handles or narrow gaps, prefer approaching above the object center first, then performing small lateral alignment motions at hover height before descending vertically. Avoid inferring insertion-side signs from ambiguous orientation vectors when a stable workspace-relative direction or fixed offset convention is sufficient.

Then, output a detailed step-by-step plan for the trajectory, including when to lower the gripper to make contact with the object, if necessary, rotation and position of the gripper, closing the gripper.
Tasks:
  pickup: after closing the gripper --> must lift the object up some distance to be considered a successful grasp 
    ) default - 50cm above the object's top surface. Smaller lifts are failures. 
    ) Special task requirements or collisions may dictate a different lift distance
  push/move: may approach the object from the side (depending on push direction) and make contact
  topple: approach the object from the side near its max height to have it fall 
  


The user command is "Open the wooden door by actuating its grey L-shaped lever handle on the front face of the door panel. Steps: (1) Compute the target as the MIDDLE of the door handle lever (the point to push down and pull). (2) Safely approach from high above the lever first to avoid hitting the door panel or the adjacent wooden post. (3) Rotate the end-effector so the gripper fingers are parallel to the lever's long axis (align gripper rotation to the handle's long-axis angle). (4) Apply a small horizontal offset along the lever's short-axis direction toward the door so that when the gripper descends, one jaw slides into the gap between the lever and the door face. (5) Descend, close the gripper to grasp the lever thickness, apply a small downward pressure to unlatch the door, then pull a large distance (perpendicular to the door face) to fully swing the door open. Beware collision with the wooden door panel and the vertical wooden post. Success = the door is visibly open (hinge angle clearly increased / door panel swung away) and the handle lever was actuated/grasped.".

- Long axis (length=0.049) orientation: `-0.028` (nearly along x-axis).

**Door panel:** There's a large panel at `[-0.25, -0.144, 0.894]` (width 0.589, the main door), and a post-like structure at `[0.005, 0.112, 0.893]`. The handle at x=-0.235 is on the large door panel. The door face is at roughly y≈0.085 (handle depth). The vertical wooden post is near x=0.005, y=0.112.

**Grasp orientation:** To grasp the lever, the gripper fingers should be parallel to the lever's long axis. Per the definition, the rotation value = the closing-motion direction, which must be aligned with the axis we grasp ACROSS. The lever thickness/short dimension is what we clamp. Since the lever is thin (height 0.009) and its short-axis (width 0.042) runs along y, we want one jaw to slide into the gap between lever and door face along the short-axis direction. 

The task says: "Rotate so gripper fingers are parallel to the lever's long axis." The long axis angle is `-0.028`. Fingers parallel to long axis → closing direction is perpendicular → rotation = short-axis angle ≈ `1.542`. This lets the jaws close across the short axis, sliding into the gap toward the door.

**Small offset toward door:** The door face/post is at larger y (~0.112) relative to handle (0.085). So the offset along short-axis (y) toward the door is in +y direction. I'll apply a small offset of ~+0.015 m in y so one jaw slides into the gap.

**Pull direction:** To swing the door open, pull perpendicular to the door face. The door face normal is roughly along y (depth). Pulling toward the robot = +y direction... but the door hinge is on the far side. I'll pull in +y (toward the robot/viewer) and slightly, combined, to swing it open. I'll pull a large distance in +y and up slightly.

**Plan:**
1. Move high above the lever middle.
2. Rotate gripper to short-axis angle (1.542), open gripper, apply small +y offset toward door.
3. Descend to lever level.
4. Close gripper on lever thickness.
5. Apply small downward pressure to unlatch.
6. Pull a large distance in +y (perpendicular to door face) to swing door open.
7. Release.

   ) Ex: If pull failed - consider changing pull direction (a little bit each attempt)
Then, replan and retry the task by continuing with INITIAL PLANNING 1.

# Analysis of Previous Failure

Looking at Attempt 1, the core issues were:

1. **Wrong target X (grasp location):** Aimed at the handle base/pivot (x=-0.235) instead of the lever's free end/middle. The correction says the true grasp middle ≈ x ≈ -0.270.
2. **Wrong offset direction:** Offset was applied in +y (away from door), but the door face is toward −y. Must offset toward −y so a jaw slides into the gap between lever and door face.
3. **Grasp too high:** z was ~0.6685, too high to straddle the lever thickness. Lower to ~0.653.
4. **Weak unlatch + pull:** Push down more, then pull in +y with a small +x arc component toward the hinge.

## Incremental change strategy
I will change **one primary thing at a time** but combine the clearly-diagnosed corrections that are all necessary for a *successful grasp* (target X, offset direction, grasp height), since without a valid grasp nothing else can be tested. I'll keep the grip rotation the same (1.542) as it was correctly aligned to the lever long axis.

---

# INITIAL PLANNING 1 — Which part to interact with

The grey L-shaped lever handle. The best interaction point is the **middle of the lever's free arm**, where the gripper can straddle the lever thickness (0.009 m tall) with one jaw sliding into the gap between the lever and the door face. Grasp along the lever's long axis (rotation = 1.542, fingers parallel to long axis).

- Lever detected center: `[-0.235, 0.085, 0.668]`, long-axis ≈ along x (-0.028), short-axis ≈ along y (1.542).
- Lever free-end/middle: `target_x = -0.270`, `target_y = 0.085`.

# INITIAL PLANNING 2 — Approach & Plan

**Approach:** Hover high above the lever middle (safe z well above door panel top ~0.72), then descend vertically after applying a small −y offset toward the door face. Avoid the wooden post at x=0.005 (well to the left of our x=-0.27 target, safe).

**Step-by-step plan:**
1. Move high above lever middle (hover z=0.80), rotation=1.542.
2. Apply −y offset toward door face at hover height.
3. Descend to pre-grasp height.
4. Descend to grasp height (jaws straddle lever), close gripper.
5. Push down to unlatch.
6. Pull door open: +y (pull toward robot) with small +x arc toward hinge.
7. Release and retreat.

```python
import numpy as np

# ---- Object-derived constants ----
handle_pos = [-0.235, 0.085, 0.668]
grip_rot = 1.542  # aligned to lever long axis (fingers parallel to long axis)

# CORRECTIONS from Attempt 1:
target_x = handle_pos[0] - 0.035   # -0.270  -> lever middle/free end
target_y = handle_pos[1] - 0.012   # 0.073   -> offset toward door face (-y)
grasp_z  = handle_pos[2] - 0.015   # 0.653   -> lower so jaws straddle lever

hover_z    = 0.80    # high safe approach above door panel top
pregrasp_z = 0.71    # just above lever
unlatch_z  = grasp_z - 0.025  # push down to unlatch

# End-effector current start
start_pose = [-0.15174489680574574, 0.4088979806833218, 0.6907744732849247, 0.0]

def make_pose(x, y, z, theta):
    return [x, y, z, theta]

logger.info(PROGRESS + f"Target lever middle: x={target_x:.3f}, y={target_y:.3f}, grasp_z={grasp_z:.3f}" + ENDC)
```

```python
# Step 1: Move from current pose to high hover above lever middle, rotate to grip_rot
hover_pose = make_pose(target_x, target_y, hover_z, grip_rot)
trajectory_1 = generate_linear_trajectory(
    "Move high above lever middle and rotate gripper to lever long-axis",
    start_pose, hover_pose, num_points=15
)
execute_trajectory(trajectory_1)

# Ensure gripper open before descent
open_gripper()
```

```python
# Step 2: Descend to pre-grasp height (vertical, above lever, with -y offset already applied)
pregrasp_pose = make_pose(target_x, target_y, pregrasp_z, grip_rot)
trajectory_2 = generate_linear_trajectory(
    "Descend vertically to pre-grasp height above lever",
    hover_pose, pregrasp_pose, num_points=8
)
execute_trajectory(trajectory_2)
```

```python
# Step 3: Descend to grasp height so jaws straddle the lever thickness
grasp_pose = make_pose(target_x, target_y, grasp_z, grip_rot)
trajectory_3 = generate_linear_trajectory(
    "Descend to grasp height, jaws straddle lever",
    pregrasp_pose, grasp_pose, num_points=6
)
execute_trajectory(trajectory_3)

# Close gripper to grasp lever thickness
close_gripper()
```

```python
# Step 4: Apply downward pressure to unlatch the door
unlatch_pose = make_pose(target_x, target_y, unlatch_z, grip_rot)
trajectory_4 = generate_linear_trajectory(
    "Push lever down to unlatch the door",
    grasp_pose, unlatch_pose, num_points=6
)
execute_trajectory(trajectory_4)
```

```python
# Step 5: Pull the door open.
# Door face normal (pull direction) is +y (toward robot), with a small +x arc toward the hinge.
# Keep grasp low; pull a large distance in +y and modest +x.
pull_dx = 0.12   # arc toward hinge (+x)
pull_dy = 0.30   # perpendicular pull away from door face (+y toward robot)

# Intermediate waypoint for a smooth arc (first mostly pull-out, slight rise to avoid dragging)
pull_mid_pose = make_pose(target_x + pull_dx*0.4,
                          target_y + pull_dy*0.5,
                          unlatch_z + 0.01,
                          grip_rot)
trajectory_5 = generate_linear_trajectory(
    "Begin swinging door open: pull +y with slight +x arc",
    unlatch_pose, pull_mid_pose, num_points=10
)
execute_trajectory(trajectory_5)

# Complete the pull to fully swing the door open
pull_end_pose = make_pose(target_x + pull_dx,
                          target_y + pull_dy,
                          unlatch_z + 0.02,
                          grip_rot)
trajectory_6 = generate_linear_trajectory(
    "Complete door swing: full pull perpendicular to door face toward hinge",
    pull_mid_pose, pull_end_pose, num_points=12
)
execute_trajectory(trajectory_6)
```

```python
# Step 6: Release the handle and retreat upward to clear the door
open_gripper()

retreat_pose = make_pose(pull_end_pose[0], pull_end_pose[1], pull_end_pose[2] + 0.15, grip_rot)
trajectory_7 = generate_linear_trajectory(
    "Retreat upward after opening door",
    pull_end_pose, retreat_pose, num_points=8
)
execute_trajectory(trajectory_7)

logger.info(PROGRESS + "Door open sequence complete." + ENDC)
task_completed()
```
